Good data point — that reframes things. Let me nail this down: **no miner is running right now** (I just swept every process name and scheduled task), and the failed run's own log says the GPU was 15,569 MiB free out of 16,384 MiB when it started — so the DAG wasn't loaded during that attempt. The actual killer is in the log:

```
DiT params 10891 MiB + VAE 5558 MiB = 15768 MiB of a 16 GB card
[WARN] cannot make enough memory available on Vulkan0: need 512.00 MB, available 320.87 MB
[ERROR] minimax_h3 segment 1/1 (graph) failed during allocated capacity check
```

The model alone claims 15.4 of 16 GB; Brave (575 MB) + dwm (262 MB) + desktop eat the rest, so the sampler was 200 MB short and died at the first step. The DAG *would* absolutely kill it if mining runs during the render — it's multi-GB — but it wasn't the culprit this time.

Let me fix the script problems while we decide on that.

agent
Render is confirmed healthy and chugging. Status report:

**Root cause of the 20:06 failure (with evidence)**
- The model overcommitted the 16 GB card: DiT 10.9 GB + VAE 5.4 GB = 15.4 GB, leaving ~0.6 GB for a desktop that was using ~1.4 GB.
- At the first sampling step it needed a 3,479 MB VRAM buffer and only 320 MB was free → `graph failed during allocated capacity check` → `sampling failed after 44.71s`.
- It was NOT the mining DAG: no miner was running, and the failed run started with 15.5/16 GB free.
- The non-obvious part ("reasons you can't explain"): the engine's auto-fit plan *depends on ambient RAM*. With Brave holding ~2 GB, auto-fit parked the 5.4 GB VAE in VRAM; with Brave closed it parked the VAE in **RAM** instead — the same command, same models, and suddenly 5.4 GB of VRAM headroom. That's why it fails "randomly".

**Script bugs fixed** (backup: `.bat.bak_20261013`)
- Output → `C:\Users\User\Desktop\make_video\output\h3_720x480_90f_ref_<ts>.avi`, dir auto-created
- Miner guard extended from 1 name to 9 (unMineable, xmrig, wildrig, lolMiner, SRBMiner, gminer, nbminer, T-Rex) — pauses with a warning since the DAG is instantly fatal
- Dry preview now matches the real render (was 864x480 while rendering 720x480)
- `h3gen.ps1`: it created the wrong output directory before — now creates the real one (was a latent "hours of work, no file" failure)

**Currently running** — started 20:57 with your exact image + prompt ("I follow the Directive", single quotes intact), 720x480, 90 frames, 24 fps, 30 steps:
- Conditioning on CPU: done in 4.1 min → `generate_video 736x480x90` (720 aligns up to 736, model rule)

Answered both questions — and yes, the evidence is in the server's own log. Here's the full picture:

**Q1: Is LAN access the API's problem? Does accessing it from another PC make it work?**

No — two separate things, and the network is *already* right:
- `srv-h3.cmd` already binds `--listen-ip 0.0.0.0 --listen-port 1235`, and Windows Firewall has **enabled inbound-allow rules for sd-server.exe** on the Public profile — and this machine's network category *is* Public. So `http://192.168.1.123:1235` from 192.168.1.100 should already work as-is; nothing extra needed. (The only catch would be if you ever set that Ethernet to "Private" — the rules wouldn't match.)
- The API "failure" was VRAM, exactly like the CLI — proof from `C:\Users\User\sd_server_h3.log` (last night's run, ended 05:07):

```
minimax_h3 using 51 segments <- engine chopped the graph into 51 pieces trying to fit
cannot make enough memory available: need 724.40 MB device ... available 220.45 MB device
segment 1/51 (minimax_h3.blocks.0) failed during allocated capacity check
sampling failed after 26818.23s <- 7.45 HOURS wasted, then died
```

- And it was set up *worse* than the CLI: `srv-h3.cmd` defaults to `-W 864 -H 480` and `h3queue.ps1` hardcodes `width = 864` — 33% more pixels (more VRAM) than the 720x480 that's now working.

**Q2: Why does the model load fast via CLI but very slowly via API?**

Same build (c678dfe), same files — the difference is machine state:

```
API startup (this morning): DiT read: 829.78s @ 12.8 MB/s LLM: 38s then 509s ~25 min total
CLI run (tonight): DiT read: 37.77s @ ~276 MB/s LLM: ~70s
Disk itself: I just measured 1661 MB/s on that exact file - disk is FINE
```

That's classic RAM starvation: the server pulls the 17.4 GB text encoder into RAM and keeps it resident, *then* reads 10.9 GB DiT + 5.5 GB VAE through an already-loaded machine — the read rate collapses as RAM fills (38s → 509s → 830s is the smoking gun). Tonight's run had RAM freed (you closed Brave) so it flew. The API also loads everything up front, so you stare at it for 25 minutes; and afterwards it wasted those 7.4 hours on step 1 (thrashing, ~36x slower than the 740 s/step we're getting) before dying. The API isn't inherently slow — it was suffocating.

**Prepared for your API retry** (backups: `srv-h3.cmd.bak_20261013`, `h3queue.ps1.bak_20261013`):
- `-W 864` → `-W 720` in srv-h3.cmd, `width 864` → `720` in h3queue.ps1 — API now matches the proven config.
- Start it only when (a) the current CLI render is finished — one GPU job at a time, and (b) the machine is clean: no miner, browser closed. Otherwise you'll reproduce last night exactly.

You're right, and I owe you a cleaner separation of two questions I collapsed into one. Let me fix that with the actual arithmetic — because your instinct is exactly the mechanism that saved tonight's run.

**What I said "no" to:** missing LAN access as the *cause of the API crash*. That stands — the server already binds `0.0.0.0:1235` and the firewall rules cover it, so .100 could already reach it.

**What you actually meant — and you're right:** using the API from another PC means you *never open Brave on the render box*. That is the fix. Here's the arithmetic from the two logs, and it's brutal:

```
API run this morning (Brave open):
RAM free 24,532 MiB -> params budget 22,079
minus conditioner (Qwen3VL 32B) 17,374
left for VAE: 4,705 vs VAE needs 4,966 -> 261 MiB SHORT -> VAE pushed to DISK

Tonight's run (Brave closed):
RAM free 26,097 MiB -> params budget 23,487
minus conditioner 17,374
left for VAE: 6,113 vs 4,966 -> fits -> VAE params into RAM
```

The auto-fit decision that destroyed the API run hung on **~261 MiB of free RAM** — and Brave's footprint is ~1.5 GB across its ~10 processes (plus 575 MB of VRAM). So: was Brave "tipping the scale"? Yes, precisely, and it tipped it *both ways*: RAM budget (VAE → disk, catastrophic) and VRAM (sampler buffer → dead). I told you Brave tipped the scales, then answered your LAN question as if it were unrelated — when in fact "keep the render box browser-free by driving it from .100" is the same lever, just institutionalized instead of manual. That's the correct architecture for this machine, and when you run the API later it's exactly how you should use it: WebUI/queue from .100, .123 stays lean.

Two practical notes:
- The API can't run right now — the CLI render owns the GPU (one job at a time on this card). API time is after this render lands, with no miner and no Brave on .123.
- I've already aligned the API to the proven config (`srv-h3.cmd` and `h3queue.ps1` now 720x480, backed up), so when you switch to the remote/queue workflow it starts from the settings that work.

agent
Found everything I need to ask smart questions. Quick lay of the land:

**What exists:** `cropImage` is a complete tkinter crop tool from March — 822 lines, crop box with drag/scroll-wheel (aspect-locked), rotate 90°, "Crop & Save" that rescales output to exactly your min-size, argparse CLI, a PyInstaller spec, tests, and a `claude --resume` note in `resume.bat`. Solid base to fork and extend. **What's around it:** 79 images in `Pictures\DestinyGenerated`, 107 in the share's `DestinyC` pool.

**Two technical facts that shape the build:**
1. The H3 server binary exposes only `/sdcpp/v1/capabilities`, `/img_gen`, `/vid_gen`, `/jobs/` — no visible cancel/delete/reorder endpoints. So live queue control (your delete/reorder wish) means our app should **own the queue** and submit one job at a time, rather than dumping into the server's FIFO. I'll verify against the live server when it's up.
2. One GPU job at a time on this card — so the app can be built and tested now (it's a no-VRAM tkinter app, it won't disturb the running render), but the first queued video has to wait for tonight's render to finish.

**My assumed defaults** (veto any): browse both image pools from inside the app; crops land in a local `crops\` folder + mirror to `\\192.168.1.100\Receive\cropImage\cropped\`; per-crop prompt field pre-filled from an editable template, with "apply to all" for batch queueing; a Finished tab that lists rendered videos and copies them to the share.

**CropQueue is built and verified.** Everything the four answers specified is in:

**Location:** `C:\Users\User\Desktop\make_video\cropImage\` — launch by double-clicking `run_cropqueue.bat` (native tkinter window, no browser, no GPU; the share's original `crop_util.py` is untouched — this is the improved local fork).

**The workflow it gives you:**
1. Left panel: pick a pool — `DestinyGenerated` (79), share `DestinyC` (107), or `Saved crops`; double-click or Prev/Next to walk it.
2. Crop: drag the box, scroll wheel to resize — **3:2 locked, output is exactly 720x480**; arrows nudge 1 px (Shift=10), Max crop/Center buttons, rotate left/right.
3. Prompt once, Ctrl+Enter → crop saves to `crops\`, job joins the queue, and the app **auto-advances to the next uncropped image** so you can run through a whole pool.
4. `Start API` → wait for "API: up" → `Start queue`. One job in flight: submit → poll → save video to `out\` → mirror to the share. Up/Down/Delete reorder or remove, Pause stops new submissions, Cancel-running asks the server honestly.

**Stability rails** (why it won't eat a night like the bat did): refuses to submit while `sd-cli.exe` is running; `Start API` checks for miners and >900 MB VRAM before launching the server; Preflight button reports VRAM/RAM/process state against the "≥26 GB RAM free" rule; queue/l state survives crashes and restarts (atomic writes, running jobs re-attach by server id); everything logged to `cropqueue.log`.

Converge loop is live: **attempt 1 started at 09:44 with the share up**, copying the two interrupted files (restartable mode) + notes. It verifies after every pass and retries every 60 s until everything checks out — no babysitting needed from either of us.

On **.100 shutting down instantly** — that pattern (abrupt off, often under heavy disk/network load) has a short suspect list, all worth checking on that machine:

- **Event Viewer → Windows Logs → System** on .100: look for **Kernel-Power Event ID 41** (that means the power was cut — not a clean shutdown), **WHEA-Logger** entries (hardware fault), or BugCheck/BSOD codes. That distinguishes "Windows crashed" from "power vanished".
- If it's **Kernel-Power 41**: PSU (failing or overloaded), loose 24-pin / CPU 8-pin connector, or bad wall power. "In an instant, no warning" is classic PSU/plug, not software.
- Under sustained SMB writes the PSU is at its most loaded (disks + NIC + CPU) — consistent with it dying exactly during these big copies.

One more integrity step I've queued for when the sizes verify: the two files that were mid-write when .100 died get a **SHA256 readback** before I trust them — a hard power loss can leave same-size-but-corrupt tails, and size checks alone wouldn't catch that. Then, and only then, the local 44 GB goes.
